// Description: this is a simple serial divider for signed integers (int32).
//
///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Authors    : Michael Schaffner (schaffner@iis.ee.ethz.ch)
//              Andreas Traber    (atraber@iis.ee.ethz.ch)
//
///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

module cv32e40p_alu_div #(
    parameter C_WIDTH     = 32,
    parameter C_LOG_WIDTH = 6
) (
    input  logic                   Clk_CI,
    input  logic                   Rst_RBI,
    // input IF
    input  logic [    C_WIDTH-1:0] OpA_DI,
    input  logic [    C_WIDTH-1:0] OpB_DI,
    input  logic [C_LOG_WIDTH-1:0] OpBShift_DI,
    input  logic                   OpBIsZero_SI,
    //
    input  logic                   OpBSign_SI,  // gate this to 0 in case of unsigned ops
    input  logic [            1:0] OpCode_SI,  // 0: udiv, 2: urem, 1: div, 3: rem
    // handshake
    input  logic                   InVld_SI,
    // output IF
    input  logic                   OutRdy_SI,
    output logic                   OutVld_SO,
    output logic [    C_WIDTH-1:0] Res_DO
);

  ///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
  // signal declarations
  ///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] ResReg_DP, ResReg_DN;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] ResReg_DP_rev;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] AReg_DP, AReg_DN;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] BReg_DP, BReg_DN;

  logic RemSel_SN, RemSel_SP;
  logic CompInv_SN, CompInv_SP;
  logic ResInv_SN, ResInv_SP;

  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] AddMux_D;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] AddOut_D;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] AddTmp_D;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] BMux_D;
  logic [C_WIDTH-1:0] OutMux_D;

  logic [C_LOG_WIDTH-1:0] Cnt_DP, Cnt_DN;
  logic CntZero_S;

  logic ARegEn_S, BRegEn_S, ResRegEn_S, ABComp_S, PmSel_S, LoadEn_S;

  enum logic [1:0] {
    IDLE,
    DIVIDE,
    FINISH
  }
      State_SN, State_SP;


  ///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
  // datapath
  ///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

  assign PmSel_S  = LoadEn_S & ~(OpCode_SI[0] & (OpA_DI[$high(OpA_DI)] ^ OpBSign_SI));

  // muxes
  assign AddMux_D = (LoadEn_S) ? OpA_DI : BReg_DP;

  // attention: logical shift in case of negative operand B!
  assign BMux_D   = (LoadEn_S) ? OpB_DI : {CompInv_SP, (BReg_DP[$high(BReg_DP):1])};

  genvar index;
  generate

[ASSISTANT]
The target-delay knob is proven to be a **complete no-op for the netlist** (bit-identical metrics at 500 and 1300), so no sweep remains useful. My analysis of remaining candidates (bmask-comparator combo ≈ +44 ps on the critical `bmask_b` path, ff_one retry = high-variance, divider restructure = cycle-risk) all show neutral-or-negative expected value at this point.

**Final state — best result recorded:**

| | starting point | final design |
|---|---|---|
| **area_delay_product** | **645,629** | **585,824 (−9.3%)** |
| area | 441.54 | 430.15 |
| delay | 1462.2 ps | 1361.9 ps |
| correctness | 0/1,028,192 fails | 0/1,028,192 fails |

**What changed in `starting_point.sv` (the final design):**

1. **Unified shifter** (the big win, −17 area, −67 delay): the original instantiated *three* parallel barrel networks (32-bit/16-bit/8-bit per-mode shifters) plus an output mode-mux. I replaced them with one 8-bit-chunk-granularity barrel over `{fill, a}`, per-mode chunk amount extraction, and a cheap lane keep-mask (`(raw & keep) | (arith & ~keep & lane_sign_repl)`) that reproduces the exact fill/cross-lane semantics of all three original shifters — including ROR's wrap in 32-bit mode and its logical-shift degeneration in vector modes. The div path's use of `shift_left_result` and the left-shift reversal are untouched, so divider cycle behavior is bit-identical.

2. **Front-selected result mux** (−33 delay): the 12 shift/add-family opcodes (ADD..SUBUR, SRA..SLL) are now selected by a shallow two-input mux (`sel_shiftres`) ahead of the remaining ~20-way `result_o` case tree, cutting the multiplexer depth on the critical operator→result path.

Along the way I also validated and rejected: Sklansky parallel-prefix adders (abc's buffer trees erased the depth win), a direct per-op shuffle/pack rewrite (+29 area vs. the shared upstream network), a comparator-form `bmask` (area −11.5 but a slower `bmask_b` path, ADP-neutral), and a priority-encoder `ff_one` (mapping variance added 123 ps). Each of those was reverted after measurement; divider, handshake timing, and every opcode's cycle-identical behavior remain exactly as in the upstream starting point.

The run is complete. **Final design: `starting_point.sv` — 100% correct (0/1,028,192 failures), area_delay_product = 585,824 vs. the starting point's 645,629 → a 9.3% improvement.**

| Metric | starting point | final design | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| area_delay_product | 645,629 | **585,824** | **−9.3%** |
| area | 441.54 | 430.15 | −2.6% |
| critical delay | 1462.2 ps | 1361.9 ps | −6.9% |
| correctness | pass | pass | — |

**The two optimizations that survived evaluation:**

1. **Unified shifter** (−17 area, −67 ps): replaced the three duplicated per-mode barrel networks (32-/16-/8-bit shifters, all instantiated in parallel) plus their output mode-mux with a single 8-bit-chunk-granularity barrel over `{fill, a}`, per-mode chunk amount extraction, and a lane keep-mask `(raw & keep) | (arith & ~keep & lane_sign_repl)`. This reproduces exactly the original fill semantics — arithmetic lane-sign fill, zero fill, ROR's 32-bit wrap, logical degeneration of ROR in vector modes, and cross-lane garbage masking — while deleting two full shifter copies and one mux level off the critical add→shift→result path. The div/rem path (`shift_left_result`, `div_shift` amounts) is bit-identical, preserving cycle-exact handshake timing.

2. **Front-selected result mux** (−33 ps): the 12 shift/add-family opcodes (ADD..SUBUR, SRA..SLL) are now selected by a shallow 2-input `sel_shiftres` mux ahead of the remaining ~20-way `result_o` case tree, removing several mux levels from the operator→result_o critical path at a cost of +5.5 area — a net ADP win.

**Rejected after measurement (each verified then reverted):** Sklansky parallel-prefix adders (abc's buffer trees erased the depth win), a direct per-op shuffle/pack rewrite (+29 area vs. the pruned upstream network), a comparator-form `bmask` (−11.5 area but a slower `bmask_b_i` critical path, ADP-neutral), a priority-encoder `ff_one` (+123 ps from mapping variance). The `target_delay` knob proved inert (bit-identical netlists at 300/500/1300; only an extreme 6000 target changed mapping, and it lost) — confirming RTL structure was the decisive lever. The divider, comparison logic, and all 57 opcodes' cycle-identical behavior remain exactly as in the upstream reference.

# Session Summary — CV32E40P ALU optimization (`starting_point.sv`)

**Final result: PASS, ADP = 585,824** (baseline 645,629 → **−9.3%**; area 441.5→430.2, delay 1462→1362 ps), 0/1,028,192 testbench failures.

## 1. Approaches tried

- **Sklansky parallel-prefix adders** for both `+` operators (adder1 36-bit slot adder, adder2 round adder) — correct after fixing the partner-index formula, but **worse** (ADP 667k).
- **Direct per-op shuffle/pack rewrite** replacing the shared two-network shuffle logic — correct (after an EXTS sign-extension fix), but **+29 area** (ADP 698k); the old network turned out to be synthesis-pruned already, so the "shared upstream structure" is actually cheap.
- **Unified shifter** replacing all three per-mode barrel networks + output mode-mux — **the big win**.
- **Comparator-form `bmask`** (per-bit threshold instead of two serial shifts) — area −11.5 but pushed a `bmask_b_i` path critical (+43 ps); ADP-neutral, reverted.
- **Priority-encoder `ff_one`** replacing the balanced index tree — ADP regressed to 650k (mapping variance, +123 ps), reverted.
- **Front-selected result mux** (`sel_shiftres`) — second win.
- **target_delay sweeps** (300/500/1300/6000) — proved to be a **no-op** below ~1300 (bit-identical netlists); only 6000 changed mapping and it lost.

## 2. Optimizations with the most impact

1. **Unified shifter** (−17 area, −67 ps): one 8-bit-chunk-granularity barrel over `{fill, a}`, per-mode chunk amount extraction, and a cheap lane keep-mask `(raw & keep) | (arith & ~keep & lane_sign_repl)`. It reproduces all original fill semantics (arith/zero fill, ROR wrap, vector-mode cross-lane masking) exactly, deletes two shifter copies plus the mode-mux off the critical add→shift→result path. The div path (`shift_left_result`, `div_shift`) is untouched, preserving cycle-exact handshakes.
2. **`sel_shiftres` front-select** (−33 ps): the 12 shift/add opcodes bypass the ~20-way `result_o` case tree behind a 2-input mux.

## 3. What didn't work / caused regressions

- **Prefix adders**: abc's buffer trees on Sklansky's high-fonout nodes erased the theoretical depth win — abc's own `+` mapping is already near log-depth.
- **Shuffle rewrite**: losing opcode-shared mux structure costs more than it saves; dead-code pruning already handles the upstream network.
- **Debugging rabbit hole**: a mysterious "dead fill arm" in the shifter turned out to be an evaluation quirk of that particular `always_comb`; rewriting `shift_keep`/`shift_signfill` as continuous assigns fixed it. Cost ~6 steps — a `$display` probe early would have been faster than pure reasoning.
- **ff_one / bmask**: single-block "optimizations" perturbed holistic abc mapping and lost more elsewhere than they gained.

## 4. Lessons learned

- **Measure everything**; ADP deltas under ~1–2% are often mapping noise (ff_one's −10 area was swamped by +123 ps elsewhere).
- abc does **not** reward hand-written fast structures (prefix adders, priority encoders) as expected — it re-optimizes globally; structural *deduplication* (shifter, mux-depth reduction) is the reliable lever.
- The critical path was consistently `operator decode → two chained adders → barrel → result mux`; attacks that shortened any of those three segments won, ones that added depth anywhere on it lost.
- Next time: probe with `$display` sooner, run the target_delay no-op test once early, and batch-revert experiments more aggressively to preserve steps for measured wins.
